Choose an orthonormal basis $v_1,\ldots,v_{21}$. An equivariant map $F:V_{\mathbb C}\to\Lambda^kV_{\mathbb C}$ is determined by $F(v_1)$, because the orthogonal orbit of $v_1$ spans $V_{\mathbb C}$. Its value is fixed by all determinant-one diagonal sign changes on $v_2,\ldots,v_{21}$. A basis exterior monomial fixed by all these sign changes contains either none or all of these twenty vectors. For even $k$, only $k=0$ and $k=20$ can therefore occur. The degree-zero map vanishes, since a determinant-one orthogonal transformation sending $v_1$ to $-v_1$ forces $F(v_1)=F(-v_1)=-F(v_1)$. For $k=20$ the possible value is a multiple of $v_2\wedge\cdots\wedge v_{21}$, and volume duality $\Lambda^{20}V_{\mathbb C}\simeq V_{\mathbb C}$ supplies a nonzero equivariant map. The multiplicity is exactly one. The last assertion follows from irreducibility and the distinct dimensions $21$, $2^{10}$, and $1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Independent target operations and rational descent

<a id="stmt-gen-generation"></a>
<div id="gen:generation" class="theorem">

**Theorem 15**. *Assume Equation <a href="#gen:full-group" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:full-group">[gen:full-group]</a>. Suppose that $\alpha\in\operatorname{CH}^2(S\times B)_{\mathbb Q}$ induces a nonzero map $V\to H^2(B,\mathbb Q)$ for some complex abelian variety $B$. Let $\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2\in\{+,-\}$, and let $A_1,A_2$ be abelian varieties realizing $W^{\epsilon_1},W^{\epsilon_2}$ as their first rational cohomology. Every rational Hodge map $$\beta:V\longrightarrow W^{\epsilon_1}\otimes W^{\epsilon_2}
 \subset H^2(A_1\times A_2,\mathbb Q)$$ is the restriction of the action of a cycle $\Gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^2(S\times A_1\times A_2)_{\mathbb Q}$. In particular, this recovers the full primitive Clifford multiplication tensor and its prescribed even-Clifford realization.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-gen-generation"><summary>Proof of stmt-gen-generation</summary>

*Proof.* Pull back $\alpha$ along addition $B\times B\to B$ and apply the algebraic projector to $U\otimes U\subset H^2(B\times B,\mathbb Q)$ from Lemma <a href="#cyc:projectors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:projectors">5</a>. The resulting codimension-two cycle induces $$\alpha':V\longrightarrow U\otimes U.$$ It is nonzero: on $H^2(B,\mathbb Q)=\Lambda^2U$, the mixed component of addition pullback is the injective map $u\wedge v\mapsto u\otimes v-v\otimes u$.

<a id="gen:initial-tensor"></a>
Fix a nonzero $\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}$-equivariant map $\gamma:V_{\mathbb C}\to\Delta\otimes\Delta$. The map $\alpha'$ is rational and Hodge, hence $\mathfrak g$-equivariant. Lemmas <a href="#gen:width" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:width">13</a> and <a href="#gen:vector-multiplicity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:vector-multiplicity">14</a> give $$\label{gen:initial-tensor}
 \alpha'_{\mathbb C}=\gamma\otimes t,
 \qquad 0\ne t\in M\otimes M.$$ In this formula the tensor factors have been regrouped so that the two spin factors come first. The other summands of $U_{\mathbb C}\otimes U_{\mathbb C}$ contain no vector copy.

Put $N_j=N_{\epsilon_j}$ for $j=1,2$. The independent operations $$a\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb C}(M,N_1),\qquad
 b\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb C}(M,N_2)$$ send $t$ to $(a\otimes b)t$, and these images span $N_1\otimes N_2$. To check this, choose $\mu,\nu\in M^\vee$ with $c=(\mu\otimes\nu)(t)\ne0$. For arbitrary $n_1\in N_1$ and $n_2\in N_2$, the maps $$a(m)=\mu(m)n_1,\qquad b(m)=\nu(m)n_2$$ send $t$ to $c\,n_1\otimes n_2$. Thus any symmetry imposed on $t$ by the original exterior square causes no restriction: the two operations are chosen independently.

<a id="gen:rational-generators"></a>
To implement these operations over $\mathbb Q$, choose rational bases $$f_1,\ldots,f_r\quad\text{of }
 \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak g}(U,W^{\epsilon_1}),\qquad
 g_1,\ldots,g_s\quad\text{of }
 \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak g}(U,W^{\epsilon_2}).$$ By Lemma <a href="#gen:width" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:width">13</a>, all these maps are Hodge, and their complex spans are the full spaces of the independent operations just used. Lemma <a href="#cyc:h1" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:h1">4</a> realizes each by a rational abelian homomorphism acting contravariantly on $H^1$; this is the classical weight-one equivalence, also stated in <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Milne](#ref-Milne), Theorem 6.8 and Corollary 6.9]. Products of their transpose-graph correspondences, followed by the mixed projector if necessary, therefore realize every map $$\label{gen:rational-generators}
 \Phi_{ij}=(f_i\otimes g_j)\alpha'$$ by a rational algebraic cycle $\Gamma_{ij}$ on $S\times A_1\times A_2$. These cycles have codimension two: the target correspondences have degree shift zero, and composition preserves the degree shift of the original surface correspondence.

Lemma <a href="#gen:vector-multiplicity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:vector-multiplicity">14</a>, Equation <a href="#gen:initial-tensor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:initial-tensor">[gen:initial-tensor]</a>, and the spanning calculation show that the complex span of the maps in Equation <a href="#gen:rational-generators" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:rational-generators">[gen:rational-generators]</a> is $$\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}}
 \bigl(V_{\mathbb C},
       W^{\epsilon_1}_{\mathbb C}\otimes
       W^{\epsilon_2}_{\mathbb C}\bigr).$$ The prescribed rational Hodge map $\beta$ lies in this space. Consequently the finite system $$\beta=\sum_{i=1}^r\sum_{j=1}^s c_{ij}\Phi_{ij}$$ has a solution over $\mathbb C$. All its coefficients and its right-hand side are rational, so it has a solution with $c_{ij}\in\mathbb Q$ by Lemma <a href="#cyc:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:descent">6</a>. The cycle $\Gamma=\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}\Gamma_{ij}$ has precisely the required action.

Finally, Clifford multiplication is the rational Hodge map $$V(1)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(W^+,W^-),
 \qquad x\longmapsto L_x.$$ Using a weight-one polarization to identify $(W^+)^\vee(-1)$ with $W^+$ makes it a rational Hodge map $V\to W^-\otimes W^+$. It is therefore one of the maps just realized. The fixed odd right multiplier and prescribed weight-one duality then give exactly the specified even-Clifford tensor, by Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a>. No choice of a single spin copy or undetermined scalar remains. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-gen-rationality"></a>
<div id="gen:rationality" class="remark">

*Remark 16*. The complex decompositions in Equation <a href="#gen:spin-decompositions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:spin-decompositions">[gen:spin-decompositions]</a> are used only to compute a span. The maps $f_i,g_j$ and the cycles $\Gamma_{ij}$ are rational before that computation. In particular, the argument requires neither a split rational Clifford algebra nor descent of an individual complex spin summand. It proves algebraicity of the prescribed rational tensor on the full Clifford spaces.

</div>

<a id="spec:section"></a>
### Specialization of an actual algebraic class

We first establish a specialization statement for projective analytic families. This permits the Kuga–Satake construction to be made on a marked period polydisc, where all its rational data are constant. Throughout this section, “very general” in a connected complex manifold means outside a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Hilbert parameters over an analytic base

<a id="stmt-spec-hilbert-map"></a>
<div id="spec:hilbert-map" class="lemma">

**Lemma 17** (An analytic classifying map). *Let $p:\mathcal X\to D$ be a smooth proper holomorphic family of nonempty, pure $n$-dimensional varieties over a connected complex manifold. Suppose that an embedding $$\mathcal X\hookrightarrow D\times\mathbb P^N$$ over $D$ has been given. There are a reduced complex quasi-projective scheme $H$, a smooth projective algebraic family $\mathcal U\to H$, and a holomorphic map $\eta:D\to H^{\mathrm{an}}$ such that $\mathcal X$ is the embedded analytic pullback of $\mathcal U$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-hilbert-map"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-hilbert-map</summary>

*Proof.* The embedded fibers have a common Hilbert polynomial $P$. Indeed, Riemann–Roch expresses $\chi(\mathcal X_t,\mathcal O_{\mathcal X_t}(k))$ in terms of the fiber integrals of the Chern classes of the relative tangent bundle and the hyperplane bundle. These integrals are locally constant in a smooth proper family, and $D$ is connected.

Let $\operatorname{Hilb}^{P}(\mathbb P^N)$ be the algebraic Hilbert scheme. Fixed-polynomial boundedness supplies one sufficiently large integer $k$ for which every member $Y$ has $$\dim \operatorname{im}\bigl(H^0(\mathbb P^N,\mathcal O(k))
       \longrightarrow H^0(Y,\mathcal O_Y(k))\bigr)=P(k),$$ and its degree-$k$ ideal space defines the Hilbert scheme’s closed embedding in the corresponding Grassmannian. These uniform statements precede the construction of $\eta$; see <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GrothendieckHilbert](#ref-GrothendieckHilbert), Theorem 3.2 and Proposition 3.8].

We check that $t\mapsto H^0(\mathbb P^N,\mathcal I_{\mathcal X_t}(k))$ is holomorphic. Fix $t_0\in D$. Since $\mathcal X_{t_0}$ is reduced, point evaluations separate the restrictions of degree-$k$ polynomials. Choose finitely many points whose evaluations have rank $P(k)$, and extend these points to local holomorphic sections of $p$, using its submersion property. After locally trivializing $\mathcal O(k)$ along these sections, evaluation is a holomorphic matrix. A nonzero $P(k)$-minor remains nonzero near $t_0$. Its rank cannot exceed $P(k)$, because evaluation factors through restriction to $\mathcal X_t$. Consequently its kernel equals the degree-$k$ ideal space, and these kernels give the required holomorphic Grassmannian map.

Pointwise this map lies in the Hilbert locus. Since $D$ is reduced, the defining equations of that locus vanish on $D$, so the map factors holomorphically through the Hilbert scheme. Take $H$ to be the reduction of the open locus parametrizing smooth pure $n$-dimensional subschemes, and let $\mathcal U$ be the restricted universal family. Flatness of the universal family and smoothness of all its fibers imply that $\mathcal U\to H$ is smooth. Its pullback to $D$ and $\mathcal X$ are reduced closed analytic subspaces of $D\times\mathbb P^N$ with the same fibers, hence the same underlying set. They therefore coincide. ◻

</details>

For a closed subscheme $Z$ of dimension at most $a$, write $[Z]_a$ for its $a$-dimensional fundamental cycle, with the lengths at the generic points as multiplicities. Components of smaller dimension do not enter $[Z]_a$.

<a id="stmt-spec-flat-cycle"></a>
<div id="spec:flat-cycle" class="lemma">

**Lemma 18** (Classes of flat subschemes). *Let $f:M\to R$ be a smooth projective algebraic family of relative dimension $n$, with $R$ a smooth irreducible complex variety of dimension $b$. Let $Z\subset M$ be a closed subscheme, flat over $R$, whose fibers have dimension $n-2$. Then $$r\longmapsto
 \operatorname{cl}([Z_r]_{n-2})\in H^4(M_r,\mathbb Q)$$ is a section of the local system $R^4f^{\mathrm{an}}_*\mathbb Q$. This includes nonpure and nonreduced fibers.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-flat-cycle"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-flat-cycle</summary>

<a id="spec:restriction"></a>
*Proof.* The total space $M$ is smooth. Flatness and the dimension formula give $\dim Z=n+b-2$, so its top cycle has a class $$\zeta=\operatorname{cl}([Z]_{n+b-2})\in H^4(M,\mathbb Q).$$ Here the usual cycle class on a smooth variety takes values in ordinary cohomology; completeness of $M$ is unnecessary. We claim that, for the inclusion $i_r:M_r\hookrightarrow M$, $$\label{spec:restriction}
 i_r^*\zeta=\operatorname{cl}([Z_r]_{n-2}).$$

Choose local algebraic coordinates by an *étale* map from a neighborhood $U\subset R$ to $\mathbb A^b$ taking $r$ to $0$. Shrink $U$ to remove the other points over $0$. The coordinate functions then cut out a smooth flag $$U=U_0\supset U_1\supset\cdots\supset U_b=\{r\}.$$ Put $Z_j=Z\times_R U_j$. It remains flat over $U_j$ and has dimension at most $n+b-2-j$. At each step the next coordinate is a nonzerodivisor on $U_j$ and hence on the entire scheme $Z_j$. The Cartier intersection formula therefore gives $$[Z_j]_{n+b-2-j}\longmapsto[Z_{j+1}]_{n+b-3-j},$$ with the scheme multiplicities <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[StacksCartier](#ref-StacksCartier), Lemma 42.29.5, Tag 02TB]. Applying the formula successively yields $$i_r^![Z]_{n+b-2}=[Z_r]_{n-2}.$$ The formula applies to the whole possibly nonpure or nonreduced scheme at each cut; no componentwise flatness assertion is needed. Compatibility of Betti cycle classes with these regular pullbacks proves <a href="#spec:restriction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:restriction">[spec:restriction]</a>.

Finally, the restrictions of a single cohomology class on the total space of a smooth proper family form a section of its cohomology local system. This follows directly from local differentiable triviality. Apply it to $\zeta$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-spec-specialization"></a>
<div id="spec:specialization" class="proposition">

**Proposition 19** (Specialization from very general algebraicity). *Let $D$ be a connected polydisc, and let $p:\mathcal X\to D$ be a smooth proper holomorphic family of $n$-dimensional varieties, $n\geq2$, with a relative embedding in $D\times\mathbb P^N$. Let $$\theta\in H^0(D,R^4p_*\mathbb Q)$$ be a flat rational class. If $\theta_t$ is the class of a rational codimension-two algebraic cycle for very general $t\in D$, then the same holds for every $t\in D$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-specialization"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-specialization</summary>

*Proof.* Fix a desired point $0\in D$ and trivialize $R^4p_*\mathbb Q$ over $D$. Use Lemma <a href="#spec:hilbert-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:hilbert-map">17</a> to write the family as the pullback of $\mathcal U\to H$ along $\eta:D\to H^{\mathrm{an}}$. For each possible Hilbert polynomial $Q$ of degree $n-2$, consider $$T_Q=\operatorname{Hilb}^{Q}(\mathcal U/H).$$ This is projective over $H$ and has a flat universal subscheme. It has finitely many reduced irreducible components. Resolve each component by a projective birational morphism, obtaining a finite collection of smooth irreducible varieties $R\to (T_Q)_{\mathrm{red}}$ whose images cover $(T_Q)_{\mathrm{red}}$; use <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Hironaka](#ref-Hironaka)]. Thus each composite $R\to H$ is proper. Pullback gives a smooth projective ambient family $\mathcal U_R\to R$ and a flat closed subscheme $Z_R\subset\mathcal U_R$. By Lemma <a href="#spec:flat-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:flat-cycle">18</a>, the classes of the top cycles of its fibers are sections of $R^4(\mathcal U_R/R)_*\mathbb Q$.

Form the analytic spaces $$K_R=D\times_{H^{\mathrm{an}}}R^{\mathrm{an}}.$$ They are proper over $D$. Their pulled-back ambient families agree with the pullback of $\mathcal X$, so the preceding cycle classes become locally constant functions on $K_R$ with values in the fixed marked space $H^4(\mathcal X_0,\mathbb Q)$. The spaces $K_R$ may be singular or nonreduced: this causes no difficulty, since local constancy was established on the smooth parameter $R$ before base change.

There are countably many $Q$ and finitely many choices of $R$ for each $Q$. Each $K_R$ is second countable and has a locally finite family of closed irreducible analytic components. It consequently has at most countably many such components. Every component is proper over $D$; Remmert’s Proper Mapping Theorem makes its image a closed analytic subset of $D$ <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Grauert](#ref-Grauert), 7, Satz 1]. Let $E$ be the union of the images of all components whose image is not all of $D$. Thus $E$ is a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets.

Choose $t\in D$ outside $E$ and outside the exceptional sets in the hypothesis. Such a point exists by the Baire Category Theorem. Choose an actual cycle representing $\theta_t$, and write it as a finite sum $$\gamma_t=\sum_{j=1}^{s}a_j[Y_{j,t}],
 \qquad a_j\in\mathbb Q,$$ where each $Y_{j,t}\subset\mathcal X_t$ is an integral subvariety of codimension two. Its Hilbert point lifts to one of the parameters $R$, and hence to a point $u_j\in K_R$ above $t$. Choose an irreducible component $C_j$ containing $u_j$. Because $t\notin E$, the map $C_j\to D$ is surjective. In particular, there is a point $v_j\in C_j$ above $0$.

An irreducible complex analytic space is connected. The marked cycle class is therefore constant on $C_j$, and the fiber of the universal subscheme at $v_j$ has top cycle $\delta_{j,0}$ satisfying $$\operatorname{cl}(\delta_{j,0})
 =\operatorname{cl}([Y_{j,t}])
 \quad\text{under the marking.}$$ These are algebraic cycles on $\mathcal X_0$: the fibers of the universal subscheme are algebraic in $\mathbb P^N$, and the embedded fiber identification is algebraic by GAGA <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[SerreGAGA](#ref-SerreGAGA)]. Consequently $$\gamma_0=\sum_{j=1}^{s}a_j\delta_{j,0}
 \quad\text{satisfies}\quad
 \operatorname{cl}(\gamma_0)=\theta_0.$$ The argument applies to any chosen point of $D$. ◻

</details>

The avoidance in this proof is essential: density of fibers with algebraic classes by itself would not ensure that the components parametrizing those cycles reach the desired fiber. Proposition <a href="#spec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:specialization">19</a> requires actual algebraicity at very general points. It assumes no principle asserting algebraicity from the Hodge property of a flat class.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### A marked family for the primitive Clifford tensor

<a id="stmt-spec-local"></a>
<div id="spec:local" class="lemma">

**Lemma 20** (Local polarized families). *Fix a primitively amply polarized K3 surface $(S_0,h)$. After choosing a sufficiently small full-dimensional polarized period polydisc $D$ about its period, there are smooth proper holomorphic families $$\mathcal S\longrightarrow D,
 \qquad \mathcal A\longrightarrow D,$$ with the following properties. The K3 family has central fiber $(S_0,h)$, a relative ample line bundle, and a marking of its integral second cohomology. The primitive rational local system is a fixed quadratic space $(V,q)$ of dimension $21$. The abelian family has marked rational first cohomology $W=C^+(V,q)$ with the full Clifford Hodge structures. Fixed rational polarization and parity-identification data give a flat family of standard tensor maps $$\mu_t:V\longrightarrow H^1(\mathcal A_t,\mathbb Q)
                       \otimes H^1(\mathcal A_t,\mathbb Q).$$ Finally, after shrinking $D$, the product $$\mathcal X=\mathcal S\times_D\mathcal A\times_D\mathcal A$$ admits a relative embedding in $D\times\mathbb P^N$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-local"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-local</summary>

*Proof.* Polarized deformation theory and local Torelli give the smooth K3 family over a $19$-dimensional polydisc on which the period map is a local isomorphism; see <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Huybrechts](#ref-Huybrechts), Chapter 6, Section 2]. Since $D$ is contractible, its cohomology local systems are marked. The class $h$ remains of type $(1,1)$. We recall why it is represented by a line bundle on the total family. Local differentiable triviality over the contractible base extends $h$ to $H^2(\mathcal S,\mathbb Z)$. The polydisc is Stein, and coherent Leray and Cartan’s Theorem B identify $$H^2(\mathcal S,\mathcal O_{\mathcal S})
 =H^0(D,R^2p_*\mathcal O_{\mathcal S}),
 \qquad p:\mathcal S\to D.$$ The direct image on the right is locally free and commutes with base change. The image of $h$ in it vanishes fiberwise, hence vanishes. The exponential sequence now gives a holomorphic line bundle $\mathcal L$ with first Chern class $h$.

A high power of $\mathcal L$ is very ample on $S_0$ and has vanishing higher cohomology there. Proper cohomology and base change extend a basis of its sections over a neighborhood of $0$. They give a relative embedding after shrinking: basepoint freeness and immersion persist near the central fiber, and properness excludes pairs of distinct points with the same image. For the latter assertion, any sequence of such pairs approaching the central fiber either limits to distinct points, contrary to its embedding, or to a single point, contrary to the local embedding furnished by the immersion. This proves local relative projectivity, and also that $h$ stays ample. The analytic tools used here are the proper direct-image and base-change theorems and the Stein vanishing theorem; see <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Grauert](#ref-Grauert), 6, Hauptsatz I; 7, Satze 3,5; 2, Satz 3].

On the fixed Clifford space $W$, the Hodge filtration and rational alternating polarization are those constructed in Proposition <a href="#cliff:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:construction">9</a>. To recall the holomorphic dependence, if $\omega_t$ spans the period line, then $$F^1W_{\mathbb C}
 =\operatorname{im}\bigl(L_{\omega_t}:C^-(V_{\mathbb C},q)
                                      \longrightarrow C^+(V_{\mathbb C},q)\bigr).$$ This has constant half dimension and varies holomorphically. The polarization can be fixed on a sufficiently small chart. Choose a rational symplectic basis and let its integral span be a lattice $\Lambda$ in $W$. It is unimodular for the chosen symplectic form and does not change the rational Hodge structure. Since $W$ is cohomology, the associated torus is explicitly $$\mathcal A_t=(F^1W_t)^\vee/\operatorname{image}(\Lambda^\vee),$$ where a lattice functional is restricted to $F^1W_t$. Thus $H_1(\mathcal A_t,\mathbb Z)=\Lambda^\vee$, $H^1(\mathcal A_t,\mathbb Z)=\Lambda$, and $H^{1,0}(\mathcal A_t)=F^1W_t$, compatibly with the markings. The polarization supplies the Riemann form on the dual lattice. Equivalently, the marked filtrations give a holomorphic map to Siegel space and $\mathcal A\to D$ is the pullback of its marked torus family. The full markings supply, in particular, level-$3$ data. The spin action is defined on the fixed Clifford space over the polydisc, so there is no monodromy lifting problem on this base.

For completeness, in symplectic period coordinates this torus family is $$\mathbb C^g/(\mathbb Z^g+\tau(t)\mathbb Z^g),
 \qquad \tau(t)^{\mathsf t}=\tau(t),\quad
          \operatorname{Im}\tau(t)>0.$$ The factors $$\exp\bigl(-\pi i\,n^{\mathsf t}\tau(t)n
               -2\pi i\,n^{\mathsf t}z\bigr),
 \qquad m+\tau(t)n\in\mathbb Z^g+\tau(t)\mathbb Z^g,$$ define a holomorphic theta line bundle, ample on every fiber. The preceding extension-of-sections argument gives a relative projective embedding after shrinking $D$.

Fix a rational nonisotropic vector $z_0\in V$ and the polarization identification $j:W^\vee(-1)\xrightarrow{\sim}W$. The maps $$V(1)\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}(W),
 \qquad x\longmapsto\bigl(w\longmapsto xwz_0\bigr)$$ give $\mu_t$ after untwisting and applying $j$ to the dual slot. Clifford multiplication, $z_0$, and $j$ are constant rational data in the markings. Thus $\mu_t$ is flat as a rational tensor and is a Hodge map in every fiber. A Segre embedding combines the relative embeddings of $\mathcal S$ and the two copies of $\mathcal A$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-spec-primitive"></a>
<div id="spec:primitive" class="corollary">

**Corollary 21** (The primitive map at every polarized fiber). *Fix a polarized moduli component, and assume that at very general points the nonzero algebraic input of Theorem <a href="#main:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:generic">1</a> holds. Then at every $(S_0,h)$ in that component the standard full primitive Clifford tensor is induced by a rational codimension-two algebraic cycle. The same conclusion holds if the hypothesis is made outside countably many proper closed analytic subsets in a full-dimensional polarized period chart about each desired point.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-primitive"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-primitive</summary>

<a id="spec:theta"></a>
*Proof.* Take the families in Lemma <a href="#spec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:local">20</a> about $(S_0,h)$. Under the markings, extend $\mu_t$ by zero on $\mathbb Qh$ and on the other degrees of $H^*(\mathcal S_t,\mathbb Q)$. Poincaré duality and the Künneth decomposition give a unique flat class $$\theta\in H^0(D,R^4(\mathcal X/D)_*\mathbb Q)$$ with this action. More explicitly, for a rational basis $(v_i)$ of $V$, let $(q^{ij})$ be the inverse cup-pairing matrix. Then $$\label{spec:theta}
 \theta_t=\sum_{i,j}q^{ij}v_i\otimes\mu_t(v_j)
 \ \in\ V\otimes
 \bigl(H^1(\mathcal A_t)\otimes H^1(\mathcal A_t)\bigr)
 \ \subset\ H^4(\mathcal X_t,\mathbb Q).$$

By Proposition <a href="#gen:genericity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:genericity">11</a>, after excluding countably many proper closed analytic subsets we have $\operatorname{Hg}(V_t)=\operatorname{SO}(V,q)$. At a point satisfying this condition and the assumed input, Theorem <a href="#gen:generation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:generation">15</a> produces a cycle inducing $\mu_t$ on $V_t$. Its full class can be made exactly $\theta_t$. Indeed, on $\mathcal S_t$ the algebraic correspondence <a id="spec:primitive-projector"></a>$$\label{spec:primitive-projector}
 \pi_{V,t}=[\Delta_{\mathcal S_t}]
 -[o_t]\times\mathcal S_t-\mathcal S_t\times[o_t]
 -\frac{h\times h}{\int_{\mathcal S_t}h^2},
 \qquad o_t\in\mathcal S_t,$$ acts identically on $V_t$ and kills $H^0$, $H^4$, and $\mathbb Qh$. There is no odd K3 cohomology. Precompose the chosen cycle with $\pi_{V,t}$ and postcompose with the algebraic projector onto $H^1(\mathcal A_t)\otimes H^1(\mathcal A_t)$, as in Lemma <a href="#cyc:projectors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:projectors">5</a>. These operations preserve codimension two. The resulting cycle has the prescribed action in every source degree, so Poincaré duality and Künneth identify its entire class with <a href="#spec:theta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:theta">[spec:theta]</a>. The point $o_t$ is needed only on this fiber; its cohomology class, and hence the projector’s cohomological action, is canonical.

If the hypothesis is stated in algebraic moduli, its exceptional loci pull back to proper closed analytic subsets of $D$. They remain proper because the full-dimensional polarized period chart has open image locally in that component, up to a finite automorphism quotient. Thus $\theta_t$ is algebraic very generally on $D$ in either version of the hypothesis. Proposition <a href="#spec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:specialization">19</a> produces an actual rational codimension-two cycle with class $\theta_0$. Its action on $V$ is exactly $\mu_0$. Undoing the fixed invertible right multiplier on the output Hom slot, using Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a>, also algebraically realizes $m^q$ itself. ◻

</details>

Neither the original witness abelian varieties nor their cycles have to form a family. Theorem <a href="#gen:generation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:generation">15</a> first transfers the input to the fixed local full Clifford family. The specialization argument then uses a finite representative of one sufficiently general cycle, keeping its rational coefficients unchanged.

<a id="data:section"></a>
### Restriction and the prescribed data at a special fiber

Let $(S,h)$ now be any fiber under consideration, and put $$V=h^\perp\subset H^2(S,\mathbb Q),\qquad
 T=\operatorname{NS}(S)_{\mathbb Q}^{\perp}\subset V.$$ Suppose that the primitive Clifford multiplication for the cup form $q$ on $V$ has been algebraically realized. We show that this gives the stipulated standard map on $T$, including its chosen rational quadratic polarization. The arguments use no hypothesis on the Hodge group at this fiber.

<a id="stmt-data-restriction"></a>
<div id="data:restriction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 22** (Restriction to the transcendental part). *Set $q_0=q|_T$. Algebraic realization of $m^q$ on $V$ implies algebraic realization of $m^{q_0}$ on $T$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-data-restriction"><summary>Proof of stmt-data-restriction</summary>

*Proof.* The orthogonal decomposition $V=T\oplus T^\perp$ is a decomposition of rational Hodge structures, and $T$ contains the period plane. By the Clifford Basis Theorem there are inclusions $$C^\pm(T,q_0)\hookrightarrow C^\pm(V,q).$$ They are weight-one Hodge inclusions: the element $J$ defining the left Hodge circle already belongs to $C^+(T_{\mathbb R},q_0)$. Let $i:C^+(T,q_0)\to C^+(V,q)$ be the inclusion and choose a rational Hodge retraction $p:C^-(V,q)\to C^-(T,q_0)$. Such a retraction exists by polarizability: the orthogonal complement for a weight-one polarization is a rational Hodge complement. For every $x\in T$, <a id="data:restriction-identity"></a>$$\label{data:restriction-identity}
 p\,L_x^{V}\,i=L_x^{T},$$ because $xC^+(T,q_0)\subset C^-(T,q_0)$ and $p$ restricts to the identity there. Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a> realizes the two operations on the abelian factors. Restricting the resulting cycle action to $T$ gives the asserted equality; no operation on the K3 factor is needed. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-data-polarization"></a>
<div id="data:polarization" class="proposition">

**Proposition 23** (Change of quadratic polarization). *Let $q_0$ and $q_1$ be rational quadratic Hodge polarizations on the same K3-type Hodge structure $T$, allowing either overall sign as in Section <a href="#cliff:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:section">3</a>. If $T\subset H^2(S,\mathbb Q)$ and $m^{q_0}$ is algebraically realized, then so is $m^{q_1}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-data-polarization"><summary>Proof of stmt-data-polarization</summary>

*Proof.* *Step 1: square-root isometries.* Write <a id="data:P"></a>$$\label{data:P}
 q_1(u,v)=q_0(Pu,v).$$ The two form isomorphisms from $T$ to its twisted dual show that $P$ is an invertible rational Hodge endomorphism. Symmetry of the forms makes $P$ self-adjoint for $q_0$. Let $K$ be $+1$ on the real period plane and $-1$ on the real $(1,1)$-part, and let $\epsilon_0$ be the sign of $q_0$ on the period plane. Then $$(u,v)_0=\epsilon_0 q_0(Ku,v)$$ is positive definite. Since $P$ commutes with $K$, it is also self-adjoint for this positive definite real form. Hence $P$ is semisimple with real nonzero eigenvalues. Its distinct eigenvalues $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r$ and spectral projectors $p_1,\ldots,p_r$ are defined over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, the latter being polynomials in $P$.

Choose $\nu_i\in\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with $\nu_i^2=\lambda_i$. For each $s=(s_1,\ldots,s_r)\in\{1,-1\}^r$, put <a id="data:roots"></a>$$\label{data:roots}
 R_s=\sum_{i=1}^r s_i\nu_i p_i.$$ These are invertible self-adjoint operators with $R_s^2=P$. Consequently $$q_0(R_su,R_sv)=q_0(Pu,v)=q_1(u,v).$$ Thus $R_s:(T,q_1)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}\to
(T,q_0)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}$ is an isometry and extends to a parity-preserving Clifford algebra isomorphism $$F_s:C(T,q_1)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}
          \xrightarrow{\sim}C(T,q_0)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}.$$ *Step 2: weight-one Hodge maps.* We show that the parity restrictions of $F_s$ and their inverses lie in the $\overline{\mathbb Q}$-span of rational weight-one Hodge maps.

Let $\mathcal J$ be the joint rational Hodge group of $T$ and the four weight-one spaces $P_j^\pm=C^\pm(T,q_j)$, $j=0,1$. Here the joint Hodge group is the smallest rational algebraic subgroup containing their simultaneous real Hodge circle. Its geometric identity component is defined over $\mathbb Q$ and already contains that connected circle. Minimality therefore gives $\mathcal J=\mathcal J^\circ$, so $\mathcal J$ is geometrically connected. Write $\rho_T$ for its action on $T$. Since $P$ is a rational Hodge endomorphism, it commutes with $\rho_T(\mathcal J)$; therefore every $R_s$ does as well after scalar extension.

For each $j$, the projection of $\mathcal J$ to the actions on $(T,P_j^+,P_j^-)$ lies in the image of $$\operatorname{Spin}(T,q_j)\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{GL}(T)\times\operatorname{GL}(P_j^+)
                      \times\operatorname{GL}(P_j^-),$$ using the orthogonal action on $T$ and left multiplication on the parity spaces. Indeed, this rational algebraic image contains the projected circle by <a href="#cliff:circle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cliff:circle">[cliff:circle]</a>. The representation is faithful, since the left action on $C^+$ recovers a spin element by evaluation at $1$. Thus it identifies the spin group with its algebraic image, and gives algebraic homomorphisms $$\rho_j:\mathcal J\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spin}(T,q_j)$$ covering the same action $\rho_T$.

<a id="data:lift-agreement"></a>
The isometry $R_s$ induces an isomorphism of spin groups $\widehat R_s:\operatorname{Spin}(T,q_1)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}
\to\operatorname{Spin}(T,q_0)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}$. Since $R_s$ commutes with $\rho_T$, both $\widehat R_s\rho_1$ and $\rho_0$ lift the same orthogonal representation of $\mathcal J_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}$. Their pointwise quotient therefore takes values in the central kernel $\mu_2$ of the spin covering. This quotient is a morphism from a connected group to a finite group, and is the identity at the identity. Hence $$\label{data:lift-agreement}
 \widehat R_s\rho_1=\rho_0.$$ Because $F_s$ is an algebra isomorphism, <a href="#data:lift-agreement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="data:lift-agreement">[data:lift-agreement]</a> says precisely that $F_s$ intertwines the left $\mathcal J$-actions on both parities.

<a id="data:hom-descent"></a>
For finite-dimensional rational representations, equivariant Hom-spaces commute with field extension: equivariance is a set of rational linear equations on the matrix of a map. Rational maps intertwine $\mathcal J$ exactly when they intertwine its Hodge circle. Thus $$\label{data:hom-descent}
 \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal J}(P_1^\pm,P_0^\pm)
       \otimes_{\mathbb Q}\overline{\mathbb Q}
 =\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal J_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}}
       (P_{1,\overline{\mathbb Q}}^\pm,
        P_{0,\overline{\mathbb Q}}^\pm)$$ expresses $F_s^\pm$, and likewise $(F_s^\pm)^{-1}$, as finite linear combinations of rational weight-one Hodge maps.

<a id="data:conjugation"></a>
*Step 3: recovery and rational descent.* Fix abelian realizations and rational dualities for the $q_1$ target, and let $\mathcal A$ be the rational subspace of maps $$T\longrightarrow P_1^-\otimes P_1^+$$ obtained by restricting rational codimension-two cycle actions from $S$ and projecting to this Künneth component. It is a subspace of a finite-dimensional rational Hom-space. By Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a> and <a href="#data:hom-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="data:hom-descent">[data:hom-descent]</a>, conjugating the algebraically realized $q_0$ multiplication on its Hom slots gives elements of $\mathcal A\otimes\overline{\mathbb Q}$. The exact Clifford identity is $$\label{data:conjugation}
 (F_s^-)^{-1}L_x^{q_0}F_s^+
       =L_{R_s^{-1}x}^{q_1}\qquad(x\in T).$$ Here the conjugation is expanded using rational Hodge maps on the two abelian factors; it does not apply $R_s^{-1}$ to the cohomology of $S$ by a correspondence.

<a id="data:sign-average"></a>
An elementary sign average recovers the unmodified source: $$\label{data:sign-average}
 p_i=\frac{\nu_i}{2^r}
        \sum_{s\in\{1,-1\}^r}s_iR_s^{-1},
 \qquad \operatorname{id}_T=\sum_{i=1}^r p_i.$$ Indeed $R_s^{-1}=\sum_j s_j\nu_j^{-1}p_j$ and $\sum_s s_i s_j=2^r\delta_{ij}$. Equations <a href="#data:conjugation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="data:conjugation">[data:conjugation]</a> and <a href="#data:sign-average" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="data:sign-average">[data:sign-average]</a> therefore place the tensor realization of $x\mapsto L_x^{q_1}$ in $\mathcal A\otimes\overline{\mathbb Q}$. This map is rational, so Lemma <a href="#cyc:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:descent">6</a> places it in $\mathcal A$ itself. A finite rational linear combination of actual cycles realizes it. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-data-scope"></a>
<div id="data:scope" class="remark">

*Remark 24*. The proof never uses an algebraic correspondence representing $P$, any $p_i$, or any $R_s$ on the K3 surface. They enter only the identities that produce weight-one intertwiners and the finite rational descent. In particular $P$ need not be scalar. The argument applies with additional real multiplication or complex multiplication endomorphisms, and requires no description of the special Hodge group. It applies also in ranks $2$, $3$, and $4$. In rank $2$, the full even-Clifford space is two-dimensional and $\operatorname{Spin}_2\to\operatorname{SO}_2$, over an algebraic closure, is the squaring map on $\mathbb G_m$ with kernel $\mu_2$. The faithful left Clifford action and the connected-lift argument remain valid. Higher small ranks introduce no exception. We make no assertion that all Hodge embeddings for a smaller special Hodge group are generated by these operations.

The opposite-sign case is directly visible in rank two. Take $q_0=\operatorname{diag}(1,1)$ and $q_1=-q_0$ in the same period basis. Then $J_1=-e_1e_2$ and the isometry $R=i\operatorname{id}$ induces $F(J_1)=-(ie_1)(ie_2)=J_0$. Thus the nonreal square root preserves the specified weight-one circle, as the lift argument predicts.

</div>

For completeness, a rational Hodge automorphism $D$ of $T$ in a prescribed identification can also be incorporated without applying it as a K3 correspondence. First use Proposition <a href="#data:polarization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="data:polarization">23</a> for the pullback form $D^*q_1$. The rational isometry $D:(T,D^*q_1)\to(T,q_1)$ induces weight-one Hodge isomorphisms on Clifford spaces: the same connected-lift argument proves this, since $D$ commutes with the joint Hodge action. Transport on the two Hom slots then gives exactly $x\mapsto L_{Dx}^{q_1}$.

Finally apply Proposition <a href="#data:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="data:restriction">22</a>, then Proposition <a href="#data:polarization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="data:polarization">23</a>, and then the prescribed right multiplier and duality of <a href="#cliff:standard" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cliff:standard">[cliff:standard]</a>. Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a> transports the resulting tensor to the actual $H^1(A_S,\mathbb Q)$ model. The target Künneth projectors give a cycle $$\Gamma_S\in\operatorname{CH}^2(S\times A_S\times A_S)_{\mathbb Q}
 \quad\text{with}\quad
 (\Gamma_S)_*x=\kappa_S(x)\quad\text{for every }x\in T.$$ This is equality with the prescribed full map, including its rational normalization, and completes the exact-data step in Theorems <a href="#main:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:generic">1</a> and <a href="#main:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:specialization">2</a>.

<a id="sec:conclusion"></a>
### Completion of the reductions

<details id="proof-stmt-data-scope"><summary>Proof of stmt-data-scope</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#main:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:generic">1</a>.* Proposition <a href="#gen:genericity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:genericity">11</a> gives $T=V$. Proposition <a href="#cyc:equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:equivalence">7</a> proves the equivalence of conditions <span class="upright">(i)</span> and <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. From condition <span class="upright">(i)</span>, Theorem <a href="#gen:generation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:generation">15</a> realizes the full primitive Clifford multiplication. Proposition <a href="#data:polarization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="data:polarization">23</a> and Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a> then give condition <span class="upright">(iii)</span> with the stipulated data. Conversely, condition <span class="upright">(iii)</span> gives condition <span class="upright">(i)</span> by taking $B=A_S\times A_S$ and $\alpha=\Gamma_S$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-data-scope-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-data-scope</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#main:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:specialization">2</a>.* By Proposition <a href="#cyc:equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:equivalence">7</a>, either assumed input gives the nonzero correspondence very generally. Corollary <a href="#spec:primitive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:primitive">21</a>, followed by the inverse right multiplier in Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a>, algebraically realizes $m^q$ on $V$ at the desired point. Proposition <a href="#data:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="data:restriction">22</a> gives $m^{q|_T}$ on $T$. Proposition <a href="#data:polarization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="data:polarization">23</a> and Lemma <a href="#cliff:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cliff:slots">10</a> then give the prescribed quadratic form, parity identification, duality, and isogenous realization, hence <a href="#eq:cycle-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cycle-target">[eq:cycle-target]</a>. The operations on the abelian factors preserve the degree shift, so the resulting cycle has codimension two. ◻

</details>

The conclusions include Picard jumps, extra real or complex multiplication, and the smallest possible transcendental ranks. They require the prescribed multiplication tensor at those points; they do not assert generation of every Hodge embedding for a smaller special Hodge group. The mathematical content of the reduction is conditional: producing a very-general nonzero algebraic correspondence, or equivalently the surface cover of Theorem <a href="#main:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:generic">1</a>, remains the external existence problem.

<a id="app:dependencies"></a>
### Established inputs

The following background results are the external dependencies. The representation-width argument, recovery by independent tensor operations, change of quadratic form, and specialization of the prescribed class are proved in the text. No algebraicity theorem for the Kuga–Satake class is an input. Page numbers for <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Huybrechts](#ref-Huybrechts)] refer to the linked draft.

| Established statement and hypotheses                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Reference and locator                                                                                 | Role                                                                                                           |
|:--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Established statement and hypotheses                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Reference and locator                                                                                 | Role                                                                                                           |
| For smooth projective complex varieties, Hodge decomposition, Poincaré duality, the Lefschetz theorem on $(1,1)$-classes, hard Lefschetz, and the Hodge–Riemann relations hold; pullback, Gysin maps, and products have their usual Hodge types.                                                                                                                          | , §§6.1–6.3, pp. 139–154; §§7.1–7.3, pp. 157–182.                                                     | Pairings, primitive cohomology, divisor classes, and nonvanishing calculations.                                |
| Polarizable rational Hodge structures have Hodge complements, and their rational Hodge group is reductive. The same holds for a finite joint collection.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | , Proposition 3.6 and its proof, electronic p. 27; , §7.3.1.                                          | Rational splittings and the joint Hodge-group Lie algebra.                                                     |
| In characteristic zero algebraic group homomorphisms have closed image and surjective differential onto its Lie algebra. A unipotent group has nonzero invariants in every nonzero representation. A reductive Lie algebra is the sum of its center and simple ideals.                                                                                                    | , Corollary 1.4, Theorem 4.8, §7, and Proposition 14.2; , Lectures 9 and 14.                          | The rational simple ideal above the orthogonal quotient and the commuting kernel.                              |
| A complex K3 surface has the rank-$22$ cup lattice and Hodge numbers used here. Its universal deformation is smooth of dimension $20$; the local period map is an isomorphism. Fixing a nonzero integral $(1,1)$-class cuts out the corresponding local period hypersurface and extends its line bundle.                                                                  | , Chapter 1, §§2–3; Chapter 6, Corollary 2.7 and Proposition 2.8, p. 107; §2.4, p. 108.               | Marked polarized deformation charts and the rank-$21$ primitive space.                                         |
| The full even Clifford construction of a polarized Hodge structure of K3 type is polarizable of weight one. Clifford multiplication with an invertible right multiplier gives the standard Hodge embedding.                                                                                                                                                               | , Chapter 4, Propositions 2.5–2.6 and Remark 2.7, pp. 66–68; , Propositions 5.9 and 6.3.              | The prescribed full target, Hodge types, and fixed construction data.                                          |
| Complex semisimple Lie algebras have completely reducible finite-dimensional representations and highest-weight classification. In type $B_n$ the dominant-weight coordinates and spin weights have the stated forms. The Clifford Basis Theorem and spin double covering hold in characteristic zero.                                                                    | , Lectures 14 and 18–20; §19.4, pp. 294–296; §§20.1–20.2, pp. 299–312.                                | The width bound, spin multiplicity calculation, and finite-kernel lift comparison.                             |
| The functor $H^1$ identifies complex abelian varieties up to isogeny contravariantly with polarizable rational weight-one Hodge structures. A polarized complex torus is projective.                                                                                                                                                                                      | , Theorems 6.7–6.8 and Corollary 6.9, pp. 72–73 (stated covariantly for homology).                    | Algebraic realization of every rational weight-one slot map and of the Clifford spaces.                        |
| For a smooth projective complex variety $Y$, its Albanese is an abelian variety, and its Albanese map induces an isomorphism on holomorphic one-forms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | , Chapter VI, §9.2, pp. 315–316, formulas (9.11)–(9.13).                                              | Conversion of the surface-cover input into a correspondence involving an abelian variety.                      |
| On smooth projective varieties, correspondences compose by pullback, intersection, and proper pushforward, with the indicated codimension. Cycle classes commute with these operations.                                                                                                                                                                                   | , §16.1, pp. 305–315, and §§19.1–19.2, pp. 371–385; , Lemmas 45.3.1 and 45.3.6.                       | Actual rational cycles throughout, with controlled degrees and graph transposes.                               |
| For a projective family over a noetherian base, each fixed-polynomial relative Hilbert scheme is projective and has a flat universal subscheme. One uniform sufficiently high degree gives its Grassmannian realization.                                                                                                                                                  | , Theorem 3.2, p. 260; Proposition 3.8, p. 265.                                                       | Countably many proper cycle parameter spaces and the analytic classifying map.                                 |
| Flat morphisms of finite presentation with smooth fibers are smooth. In a proper flat family the smooth-fiber locus is open: remove the proper image of the closed nonsmooth locus. High powers of an ample line bundle on a projective variety are very ample and have vanishing higher cohomology. Riemann–Roch expresses their Euler characteristics by Chern classes. | , tags 01V4, 01V8, 01VU, 0B5U; , §18.3.                                                               | The smooth ambient Hilbert family, projective embeddings, and the fixed Hilbert polynomial.                    |
| If a Cartier equation is a nonzerodivisor on a closed subscheme $Z$ of dimension at most $k+1$, its Cartier pullback takes $[Z]_{k+1}$ to the $k$-dimensional cycle of the cut, with scheme multiplicities.                                                                                                                                                               | , Lemma 42.29.5(1), tag 02TB; <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Fulton](#ref-Fulton)], Chapters 2 and 6.                                                     | Specialization of top cycles, including nonreduced and nonpure Hilbert fibers.                                 |
| A proper holomorphic map has coherent higher direct images. For a coherent sheaf flat over a reduced base, fiber cohomology is upper semicontinuous; constant dimensions give local freeness and fiber base change. Coherent sheaves on Stein spaces have vanishing higher cohomology.                                                                                    | , §6, Hauptsatz I, p. 59; §7, Sätze 3 and 5, pp. 62, 64; §2, Satz 3, p. 17, with the 1963 correction. | Extension of polarizations, local relative projective embeddings, and holomorphic Grassmannian maps.           |
| A proper smooth submersion is locally differentiably trivial. Proper holomorphic images of closed analytic sets are analytic. Irreducible components of a complex analytic set are closed and locally finite; in a second-countable space there are only countably many.                                                                                                  | , Lemma 9.2, p. 77; , §7, Satz 1, p. 60; , Chapter II, Theorem 5.3, pp. 102–103.                      | Marked flat cohomology, proper-image avoidance, and transport to the chosen fiber.                             |
| Complex quasi-projective varieties admit projective resolutions. Closed analytic subvarieties of projective space and holomorphic maps between projective varieties are algebraic. Projective coherent cohomology agrees with analytic cohomology.                                                                                                                        | , Part I, Main Theorems I and I$^*$; , Theorems 1–3, pp. 19–20, and Propositions 13–15, pp. 29–30.    | Smooth cycle parameters and surfaces; algebraicity of the resulting fiber cycles and weight-one homomorphisms. |
